## Will the Layer-Down Pipeline Trigger on This Merge?

### What `governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` watches:

The workflow triggers on `push` to `main` — specifically on paths that include **canonical governance artifacts**. The key question is: do any of the 8 files in this PR match those path filters?

Let me be direct about what this PR touches vs what the trigger watches:

| File in this PR | Canonical artifact path? | Will trigger layer-down? |
|---|---|---|
| `.github/workflows/governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` | Workflow file — **not** a canonical path | ❌ |
| `governance/CONSUMER_REPO_REGISTRY.json` | Operational registry — **not** a layered-down canonical | ❌ |
| `governance/executable/workflows/consumer-alignment.yml.template` | This **is** a canonical executable artifact | ⚠️ **Possibly yes** |
| `.github/layer-down-issue-template.md` | Governance template file | ⚠️ **Possibly yes** |
| `governance/CHANGELOG.md` | Changelog — depends on path filter | ⚠️ **Possibly yes** |
| `governance/layer-down/LAYER_DOWN_INVESTIGATION_REPORT_20260227.md` | New doc under `governance/layer-down/` | ⚠️ **Possibly yes** |
| Agent memory files | Not a canonical path | ❌ |

> I can't confirm the exact path filters without reading the current `governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` on `main` — but based on what the PR adds, **the workflow on `main` (pre-merge) does NOT have the Signal 2 step**. That step only lands once this PR is merged.

---

## The Critical Timing Issue

This is the key point you need to understand:

```
BEFORE merge:  governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml on main = Signal 1 only (issues, no dispatch)
AFTER merge:   governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml on main = Signal 1 + Signal 2 (issues + repository_dispatch)
```

**So:**
- ✅ **If this merge triggers the layer-down workflow** (because some canonical files changed), it will fire **with the old version of the workflow** (Signal 1 only, no `repository_dispatch`) — because the workflow file on `main` at the time of the push is what executes.
- ✅ **All future pushes to `main` after this merge** will use the fixed workflow with both signals.

> This is standard GitHub Actions behaviour: the workflow version that runs is the one on the **base branch at push time**, not the one in the PR branch.

---

## ✅ What IS Safe to Merge Right Now

All 5 gaps are correctly addressed. The code is clean. Specifically:

- **Gap 2 fix** (Signal 2 `repository_dispatch` step) — correctly implemented, non-blocking, passes all required `client_payload` fields
- **Gap 1 fix** (liaison fields) — all 4 repos populated correctly
- **Gap 4 fix** (`MATURION_BOT_TOKEN`) — correct token, matches all other governance workflows
- **Gap 3** — confirmed no conflict, both layer-up workflows retained ✅
- **Gap 5** — investigation report is thorough and accurate ✅

## ✅ Current Status After Merge

### 1. PR #1221 — **MERGED** ✅
Merged by `@APGI-cmy` at `2026-02-27T11:09:37Z`. The fixed `governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` is now live on `main`.

---

### 2. The `governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` Workflow — What It Does NOW

The workflow on `main` is now exactly what you want. Here's how it works:

```yaml name=governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml url=https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-foreman-governance/blob/d173334eb69e9ad75baf39bed19279f9802805c3/.github/workflows/governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml#L18-L26
on:
  push:
    branches:
      - main
    paths:
      - 'governance/canon/**'
      - 'governance/schemas/**'
      - 'governance/templates/**'
      - 'BUILD_PHILOSOPHY.md'
```

**Trigger**: Any push to `main` that touches `governance/canon/**`, `governance/schemas/**`, `governance/templates/**`, or `BUILD_PHILOSOPHY.md`.

**What it does for each consumer repo:**
1. **Signal 1** — Creates a GitHub Issue with the full `layer-down-issue-template.md` body, auto-assigned to the configured `governance_liaison` agent, labelled `governance`, `layer-down`, `high-priority`
2. **Signal 2** — Fires a `repository_dispatch` event (`governance_ripple`) which triggers `governance-ripple-sync.yml` in the consumer repo for automated drift detection + alignment PR creation

**The issue template includes explicit agent instructions** (verify, ripple, update inventory, attach PREHANDOVER proof, close). It is **not** the old dump-files workflow — it is the issue-based, agent-instructed pattern you want.

---

### 3. ⚠️ The Problem You're Seeing in the Screenshot

The 3 open PRs (#639, #640, #641) in `maturion-isms` are **`[Ripple] Propagate governance layer-down`** — these were **auto-created by the machine signal** (`governance-ripple-sync.yml`) from the old pre-fix dispatch. They all reference the same source:

- **Layer-down issue**: `maturion-isms#637`
- **Canonical commit**: `a925dd789b25f27f804b35df9019494371449aae`
- **Files updated**: 6
- **Agent File Gate**: ✅ No agent files changed → standard auto-merge path

These are **exactly the old "dump files into the repo" PRs you don't want**. They were triggered by the pre-fix `governance-ripple-sync.yml` in `maturion-isms` which still auto-creates PRs. The fix to the *dispatch* side landed — but the *consumer side* (`governance-ripple-sync.yml` in `maturion-isms`) still has the old auto-PR behaviour.

---

### 4. Root Cause of the Duplicate PRs

Three identical ripple PRs exist because the `governance_ripple` `repository_dispatch` was fired 3 times — once per run of the dispatch workflow — likely from the 3 separate workflow runs triggered before the fix landed. All 3 point at the same commit and the same issue #637.

---

### 5. What You Need to Do

| Action | Priority |
|--------|----------|
| **Close 2 of the 3 duplicate Ripple PRs** (#639 and #640) — keep only one | Immediate |
| **Investigate `governance-ripple-sync.yml` in `maturion-isms`** — this is the workflow that's creating the "dump files" PRs rather than creating an issue + agent instructions | High |
| **Check issue #637 in `maturion-isms`** — this is the correct issue-based layer-down that was created (it exists, it's open, labelled `governance`/`layer-down`) but it has no agent assigned | High |
| **Backfill the 6 historical layer-down events** documented in the investigation report §6 | Medium |

The **issue #637** in `maturion-isms` is the correct behaviour — it's the "create an issue with agent instructions" pattern. The 3 Ripple PRs running alongside it are the old pattern and should be closed/replaced by the issue-driven flow.

OK — I have the full picture now. Here's the complete diagnosis and the exact redesign needed.

## 🔍 Full Diagnosis

### The Core Problem: Two Competing Systems Doing the Same Job

Right now when a `governance_ripple` `repository_dispatch` lands in `maturion-isms`, **two things happen simultaneously**:

1. **`governance-ripple-sync.yml`** runs `align-governance.sh`, detects drift, and **creates a bot PR** that dumps files (PRs #639–641 — the ones you hate ✅ understood)
2. **The issue** (`maturion-isms#637`) also exists — but it was created by the *scheduled alignment fallback*, not the dispatch

The `governance-ripple-sync.yml` workflow was built **before** the issue-based / agent-instructed pattern was designed. It's the old auto-dump-PR machine. **It needs to be replaced entirely.**

### What You Actually Want (the Target Design)

```
governance canonical push to main
         │
         ▼
governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml (foreman-governance)
         │
         ├── Signal 1: Creates issue in EACH consumer repo
         │            ├── Auto-assigned to governance-liaison-{repo}-agent
         │            ├── Lists exactly which canon files changed
         │            └── Specific step-by-step agent instructions (see below)
         │
         └── Signal 2: repository_dispatch → governance_ripple
                       (triggers consumer-side workflow)
                                │
                                ▼
                    NEW: governance-layer-down-intake.yml (consumer repos)
                         ├── Receives the dispatch
                         ├── Finds the open layer-down issue
                         ├── Assigns it to governance-liaison agent
                         ├── Posts a comment with agent_bootstrap command
                         └── Does NOT create any PR — the agent does that
```

The agent then:
1. Bootstraps, reads contract
2. Layers down the listed canon files
3. Ripples within the repo (updates cross-references, `GOVERNANCE_ALIGNMENT_INVENTORY.json`, tier 2 & 3 artifacts)
4. Invokes CodexAdvisor for tier 2/3 alignment
5. Opens a ripple PR → **auto-merge enabled** (no agent files = passes gate)
6. If agent files detected → halt, escalate to CS2, draft PR only

---

## What Needs to Change — 3 Things

### 1. `governance-ripple-sync.yml` in consumer repos → **REPLACE**

This workflow currently auto-creates dump PRs. It needs to be **replaced** with a lightweight `governance-layer-down-intake.yml` that:
- Receives the `governance_ripple` dispatch
- Finds/creates the layer-down issue and ensures it's assigned
- Posts an agent activation comment on the issue
- **Does not touch files, does not create PRs**

### 2. `layer-down-issue-template.md` in `maturion-foreman-governance` → **REWRITE**

The current template is too generic. It needs surgical, specific agent instructions.

### 3. `consumer-alignment.yml.template` in `maturion-foreman-governance` → **REPLACE**

This template (deployed to consumer repos) is what produces `governance-ripple-sync.yml`. The template must now produce `governance-layer-down-intake.yml` instead.

Here is the proposed rewrite for `.github/layer-down-issue-template.md` in `maturion-foreman-governance`:

````markdown name=layer-down-issue-template.md url=https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-foreman-governance/blob/main/.github/layer-down-issue-template.md
> ⚠️ **MANDATORY AGENT PREFLIGHT — DO NOT SKIP**
> Before reading below, call `agent_bootstrap` with your agent ID first.

## Governance Layer-Down — Agent Action Required

**Source repo**: `APGI-cmy/maturion-foreman-governance`
**Canonical commit**: `{{COMMIT_SHA}}`
**Short SHA**: `{{SHORT_SHA}}`
**Date**: `{{TIMESTAMP}}`
**Commit message**: `{{COMMIT_MESSAGE}}`

---

### Artifacts to Layer Down

The following canonical governance files changed and must be layered into this repo:

{{FILES_LIST}}

---

### Your Instructions (governance-liaison agent)

Execute the following steps in order. Do not skip or reorder.

**Phase 1 — Preflight**
1. Call `agent_bootstrap(agent_id: "governance-liaison-{repo}-agent")` — read your full contract before proceeding.
2. Record session memory at `.agent-workspace/governance-liaison-{repo}/memory/session-NNN-YYYYMMDD.md`.

**Phase 2 — Layer Down**
3. For each file in the artifact list above:
   - Copy the file from `APGI-cmy/maturion-foreman-governance` at commit `{{COMMIT_SHA}}` into the correct path in this repo (per `GOVERNANCE_ALIGNMENT_INVENTORY.json` mapping).
   - Update the version entry in `GOVERNANCE_ALIGNMENT_INVENTORY.json` for each file.

**Phase 3 — Agent File Gate**
4. Check whether any file in the artifact list is an agent contract (`.github/agents/*.md`).
   - **If YES**: HALT. Create an escalation document at `.agent-workspace/governance-liaison-{repo}/escalation-inbox/agent-contract-escalation-{{SHORT_SHA}}.md`. Do NOT proceed. Notify CS2 (Johan Ras / @APGI-cmy) and await explicit approval before continuing.
   - **If NO**: Continue to Phase 4.

**Phase 4 — Ripple (internal synchronisation)**
5. Identify all tier 2 and tier 3 artifacts in this repo that reference the layered-down canon files.
6. Invoke CodexAdvisor agent with the following instruction:

Update cross-references, version fields, and any dependent configuration. Do not modify agent contract files. Produce a single commit with all changes."
7. Verify `GOVERNANCE_ALIGNMENT_INVENTORY.json` is fully updated.
8. Attach `PREHANDOVER_PROOF` if any executable artifacts changed.

**Phase 5 — PR and Auto-Merge**
9. Open a ripple PR titled `[Ripple] Layer-down {{SHORT_SHA}} — {repo}` targeting `main`.
10. The PR must pass the OPOJD gate (0 failures, 0 skipped, 0 warnings).
11. Enable auto-merge (squash). The PR will merge automatically once checks pass.
12. Close this issue after the ripple PR merges.

{{AGENT_SECTION}}

## The New Consumer-Side Intake Workflow (replaces `governance-ripple-sync.yml`)

```yaml name=governance-layer-down-intake.yml
name: Governance Layer-Down Intake

# PURPOSE: Receives the governance_ripple repository_dispatch from maturion-foreman-governance.
# Does NOT create PRs or copy files. Activates the governance-liaison agent via issue assignment
# and a structured activation comment. The agent does all work.
#
# Authority: LAYERING_AND_RIPPLING_AUTOMATION_STRATEGY.md v1.0.0
# Replaces: governance-ripple-sync.yml (deprecated — auto-PR dump pattern)

on:
  repository_dispatch:
    types: [governance_ripple]

  workflow_dispatch:
    inputs:
      canonical_commit:
        description: 'Canonical commit SHA (for manual backfill)'
        required: false
        type: string

permissions:
  contents: read
  issues: write

jobs:
  intake:
    name: Governance Layer-Down Intake
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest

    steps:
      - name: Extract payload
        id: payload
        run: |
          if [ "${{ github.event_name }}" = "repository_dispatch" ]; then
            echo "commit_sha=${{ github.event.client_payload.commit_sha }}" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
            echo "short_sha=${{ github.event.client_payload.short_sha }}" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
            echo "source_repo=${{ github.event.client_payload.source_repo }}" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
            echo "agent_files_changed=${{ github.event.client_payload.agent_files_changed }}" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
            echo "timestamp=${{ github.event.client_payload.timestamp }}" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
          else
            SHA="${{ github.event.inputs.canonical_commit }}"
            echo "commit_sha=${SHA:-manual}" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
            echo "short_sha=${SHA:0:8}" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
            echo "source_repo=APGI-cmy/maturion-foreman-governance" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
            echo "agent_files_changed=false" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
            echo "timestamp=$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
          fi

      - name: Find or create layer-down issue
        id: issue
        env:
          GH_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.MATURION_BOT_TOKEN }}
        run: |
          SHORT_SHA="${{ steps.payload.outputs.short_sha }}"
          COMMIT_SHA="${{ steps.payload.outputs.commit_sha }}"
          AGENT_FILES="${{ steps.payload.outputs.agent_files_changed }}"

          # Bootstrap labels
          gh label create "layer-down" --color "0075ca" --description "Governance layer-down" \
            --repo "${{ github.repository }}" 2>/dev/null || true
          gh label create "governance" --color "e4e669" --description "Governance" \
            --repo "${{ github.repository }}" 2>/dev/null || true

          # Idempotency: find existing open issue for this commit
          EXISTING=$(gh issue list \
            --repo "${{ github.repository }}" \
            --state open --label "governance" --label "layer-down" \
            --json number,title \
            --jq ".[] | select(.title | contains(\"${SHORT_SHA}\")) | .number" \
            2>/dev/null | head -1)

          if [ -n "$EXISTING" ]; then
            echo "issue_number=${EXISTING}" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
            echo "created=false" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
            echo "Existing layer-down issue found: #${EXISTING}"
          else
            # Determine agent flag note
            if [ "$AGENT_FILES" = "true" ]; then
              GATE_NOTE="⛔ **Agent contract files changed — HALT after Phase 3. Escalate to CS2 before proceeding.**"
            else
              GATE_NOTE="✅ No agent contract files changed — standard path, proceed through all phases."
            fi

            ISSUE_NUMBER=$(gh issue create \
              --repo "${{ github.repository }}" \
              --title "[Layer-Down] Govern canonical update — ${SHORT_SHA}" \
              --label "governance" --label "layer-down" --label "high-priority" \
              --assignee "governance-liaison-isms-agent" \
              --body "$(printf '> ⚠️ **MANDATORY AGENT PREFLIGHT — call agent_bootstrap first.**\n\n## Governance Layer-Down — Agent Action Required\n\n**Source**: \`%s\`\n**Commit**: \`%s\`\n**Date**: \`%s\`\n**Agent File Gate**: %s\n\n---\n\nSee the [layer-down instructions](https://github.com/%s/blob/main/.github/layer-down-issue-template.md) for full step-by-step agent protocol.\n\nThis issue will be closed automatically after the ripple PR merges.' \
                "${{ steps.payload.outputs.source_repo }}" \
                "${COMMIT_SHA}" \
                "${{ steps.payload.outputs.timestamp }}" \
                "${GATE_NOTE}" \
                "${{ steps.payload.outputs.source_repo }}")" \
              2>/dev/null | grep -o '[0-9]*$')

            echo "issue_number=${ISSUE_NUMBER}" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
            echo "created=true" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
            echo "Layer-down issue created: #${ISSUE_NUMBER}"
          fi

      - name: Post agent activation comment
        env:
          GH_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.MATURION_BOT_TOKEN }}
        run: |
          ISSUE="${{ steps.issue.outputs.issue_number }}"
          COMMIT_SHA="${{ steps.payload.outputs.commit_sha }}"
          SHORT_SHA="${{ steps.payload.outputs.short_sha }}"
          AGENT_FILES="${{ steps.payload.outputs.agent_files_changed }}"

          if [ "$AGENT_FILES" = "true" ]; then
            PHASE3="⛔ **Agent File Gate TRIGGERED** — HALT at Phase 3. Do NOT proceed to ripple until CS2 approves."
          else
            PHASE3="✅ Agent File Gate CLEAR — proceed through all phases to auto-merge."
          fi

          gh issue comment "${ISSUE}" \
            --repo "${{ github.repository }}" \
            --body "$(printf '## 🤖 Agent Activation — governance-liaison-isms-agent\n\nA \`governance_ripple\` dispatch was received. You are assigned to this issue.\n\n### Action Required\n\n\`\`\`\nagent_bootstrap(agent_id: "governance-liaison-isms-agent")\n\`\`\`\n\nRead your full contract file before proceeding.\n\n### Canonical Commit\n\`%s\`\n\n### Agent File Gate Status\n%s\n\n### Standard Operating Procedure\n1. ✅ Call agent_bootstrap\n2. ✅ Layer down canon files from commit \`%s\`\n3. %s\n4. ✅ Ripple: invoke CodexAdvisor for tier 2/3 alignment\n5. ✅ Open ripple PR — enable auto-merge\n6. ✅ Close this issue after PR merges\n\n---\n*Dispatched by \`governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml\` | Run: ${{ github.run_id }}*' \
              "${COMMIT_SHA}" \
              "${PHASE3}" \
              "${SHORT_SHA}" \
              "${PHASE3}")"
```

### What Merged — Gap-by-Gap

| Gap | Fix in This PR | Workflow Impact |
|-----|----------------|-----------------|
| **Gap 2 (CRITICAL)** | Added `repository_dispatch` (`governance_ripple`) sender step to `governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` | ✅ **This is the root fix.** Consumer repos with `governance-ripple-sync.yml` will now receive the machine signal on future canonical pushes. |
| **Gap 1** | Populated `governance_liaison` fields in `CONSUMER_REPO_REGISTRY.json` v1.2.0 for all 4 consumer repos | ✅ Future layer-down issues will now be auto-assigned to the correct liaison agent |

---

### Did the Merge Itself Trigger Downstream Workflows?

**Yes — with caveats.** Because PR #1221 was merged to `main`, the following workflows were triggered:

1. **`governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml`** — Triggered on `push` to `main`, but **only** if the commit touches `governance/canon/**`, `governance/schemas/**`, `governance/templates/**`, or `BUILD_PHILOSOPHY.md`.

   **PR #1221 touched none of these paths.** It modified:
   - `.agent-workspace/...` (memory)
   - `.github/layer-down-issue-template.md`
   - `.github/workflows/governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml`
   - `governance/CHANGELOG.md`
   - `governance/CONSUMER_REPO_REGISTRY.json`
   - `governance/executable/workflows/consumer-alignment.yml.template`
   - `governance/layer-down/LAYER_DOWN_INVESTIGATION_REPORT_20260227.md`

   **➡️ `governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` did NOT fire. No layer-down issues were dispatched to consumer repos as a result of this merge.**

2. **`governance-gate.yml`** — Triggered on `push` to `main`, validates governance directory structure. This would have run ✅ (structure checks only, no file-path filter).

3. **`foreman-governance.yml`** (`Governance Policy Validation`) — Triggered on `push` to `main` when `governance/**` or `.github/workflows/**` paths change. **This DID trigger** because `.github/workflows/governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` was modified.

4. **`fm-failure-enforcement-gate.yml`** — Triggered on `push` to `main`. Would have run.

---

### What Still Won't Work (Even After This PR)

The investigation report itself documents **6 pending historical layer-down events** that have never been dispatched. These require **manual backfill issues** — this PR does not fix them retroactively. Specifically:

| Priority | Events | Agent File? | Approver needed |
|----------|--------|-------------|-----------------|
| HIGH | Items 5 & 6 (2026-02-23, 2026-02-26 canons) | No | FM / Liaison |
| HIGH | Item 4 (2026-02-21 — `ECOSYSTEM_VOCABULARY.md` + `foreman-v2.agent.md`) | **Yes** | CS2 |
| MEDIUM | Items 1 & 2 (2026-02-04, 2026-02-08) | Yes/Mixed | CS2 |

### Consumer Repos Still Need the Template Fix

Any consumer repo that previously deployed `consumer-alignment.yml.template` (with the broken `RIPPLE_DISPATCH_TOKEN`) still has the broken version locally — the fix in this PR only updates the template in the governance repo. **A backfill layer-down issue** is needed to push the corrected template to consumer repos.

    ## Summary
    The recent PR #1221 resolved core root-causes for ripple/automation failures in layer-down automation — *but* the layer-down enforcement and remediation process still presents two critical governance gaps:

    - **Historical layer-down events (at least 6) remain undispatched** to consumer repos, despite successful fixes to the dispatch signal and registry fields.
    - **Template fixes (such as the token alias and workflow contract improvements) are not automatically backfilled** to consumer repos — layer-down relies on path triggers and will not be sent unless new canon- or template-eligible commits occur.

    **Without an explicit, testable governance agent routine for both automatic and manual backfill, the risk of silent compliance drift and wasted engineering hours remains very high.**

    ## Actions Required
    1. **Create/Update a Governance Admin Agent Routine:**
       - Agent must be empowered to:
         - Proactively scan for undispatched layer-down events (using e.g., `LAYER_DOWN_INVESTIGATION_REPORT_*.md` files or logs).
         - Validate the current sync state of all consumer repos using the latest registry entries (`CONSUMER_REPO_REGISTRY.json`).

       - Update governance agent/liaison contracts to include detection and remediation flows for any future gaps — never just fix in place, always canonize the process improvement.
       - Enforce that all investigation findings (as documented in the LAYER_DOWN_INVESTIGATION_REPORT) are resolved via completed, traceable issues/PRs in both governance and affected consumer repos.

    ## Acceptance Criteria
    - All undispatched historical layer-down events are remediated with traceable issues.
    - Automated/manual layer-down re-dispatch is supported for future recovery needs.
    - Governance agent includes formal protocol for periodic audit and sync enforcement.
    - Template/contract fixes are guaranteed to reach consumer repos regardless of commit path edge-cases.
    - No further silent mis-fires or process drifts allowed; future events must auto/notified or raise agent alerts.

    ---
    **Context and Motivation:**
    - We have lost engineering time due to silent failures in ripple and agent assignment automation, especially when registry or workflow fixes take effect but are not broadcast due to path filters.
    - Manual backfills of layer-down issues are laborious, brittle, and risk falling out of sync/governance non-compliance.
    - The current solution is mostly technical — add a governance agent to own the end-to-end reliability and audit of this process.

## End-to-End Workflow Analysis: `maturion-foreman-governance` → `maturion-isms`

### ✅ What's Working Correctly

| Component | Status | Notes |
|-----------|--------|-------|
| `governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` — Signal 1 (Issue) | ✅ | Creates layer-down issue, assigns to `governance-liaison-isms-agent` |
| `governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` — Signal 2 (`repository_dispatch`) | ✅ Fixed in PR #1221 | Sends `governance_ripple` event to `maturion-isms` |
| `governance-ripple-sync.yml` — Trigger listener | ✅ | Correctly listens on `repository_dispatch: types: [governance_ripple]` + `workflow_dispatch` |
| `governance-ripple-sync.yml` — Token | ✅ | Uses `MATURION_BOT_TOKEN \|\| github.token` for checkout and gh commands |
| `governance-ripple-sync.yml` — Idempotency | ✅ | Checks for existing `ripple/governance-sync-*` PR before creating a new one |
| `align-governance.sh` — Drift detection | ✅ | Clones canonical repo, compares against `CANON_INVENTORY.json` SHA256 hashes |
| `CONSUMER_REPO_REGISTRY.json` — isms entry | ✅ | `enabled: true`, liaison set to `governance-liaison-isms-agent` |

---

### ❌ Gaps That Will Still Break the Pipeline in isms

#### **Gap 1 — CRITICAL: `governance-liaison-isms-agent` does not exist as a GitHub user/bot**
The registry and issue assignee both use `governance-liaison-isms-agent` — but this is an **agent file name**, not a GitHub username. The `gh api` issue creation call with `assignees[]=governance-liaison-isms-agent` will **silently fail** or create an unassigned issue. The layer-down issue lands with no one responsible.

**Fix needed:** The `governance_liaison` in the registry must be a valid GitHub username (e.g. `APGI-cmy` or `maturion-bot`), not an agent file handle.

---

#### **Gap 2 — CRITICAL: `align-governance.sh` clones the governance repo anonymously (no token)**
Line 89 of the script:
```bash name=align-governance.sh url=https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/blob/cababbf785a5754ce4e9eb099e4442a30312fb25/.github/scripts/align-governance.sh#L89
git clone "https://github.com/${CANONICAL_REPO}.git" "$CANONICAL_DIR" --quiet
```
This is an **unauthenticated clone**. If the governance repo ever becomes private, or GitHub rate-limits the runner, this silently fails and exits the pipeline. It should use `MATURION_BOT_TOKEN` for the clone.

---

#### **Gap 3 — CRITICAL: Path filter on `governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` is too narrow — template fixes won't dispatch**
The dispatch trigger only fires on:
```yaml name=governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml url=https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-foreman-governance/blob/81a9bb295f6eae0e9ffee5063479eed870f4d712/.github/workflows/governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml#L18-L26
on:
  push:
    branches:
      - main
    paths:
      - 'governance/canon/**'
      - 'governance/schemas/**'
      - 'governance/templates/**'
      - 'BUILD_PHILOSOPHY.md'
```
Changes to `CONSUMER_REPO_REGISTRY.json`, workflow templates (e.g. `consumer-alignment.yml.template`), or any file outside these four paths **will never trigger a dispatch** — even when they're governance-critical. This is the exact root cause of the 6 missing historical layer-downs.

**Fix needed:** Either add `governance/executable/**` and `governance/CONSUMER_REPO_REGISTRY.json` to the path filter, or add a `workflow_dispatch` manual trigger to `governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` for recovery.

---

#### **Gap 4 — MODERATE: No `commit_message` in `repository_dispatch` payload**
The dispatch step sends `commit_sha`, `short_sha`, `timestamp`, `agent_files_changed` — but **not** `commit_message`. However, `governance-ripple-sync.yml` expects `github.event.client_payload.commit_message` (line 62). It defaults to `"unknown"` — functional but loses audit traceability.

---

#### **Gap 5 — MODERATE: `governance-ripple-sync.yml` creates the PR branch but we can't see the PR creation step**
The file was truncated at line ~320. The PR creation and branch push logic wasn't fully fetched. We need to verify the PR is created with `MATURION_BOT_TOKEN` (not `github.token`) to avoid push permission failures — which was the original token issue in isms.

---

### Summary — What Needs Fixing

| # | Gap | Severity | Where to Fix |
|---|-----|----------|--------------|
| 1 | `governance-liaison-isms-agent` is not a valid GitHub assignee | 🔴 CRITICAL | `CONSUMER_REPO_REGISTRY.json` in foreman-governance |
| 2 | Anonymous clone of governance repo in `align-governance.sh` | 🔴 CRITICAL | `align-governance.sh` in maturion-isms |
| 3 | Path filter too narrow — non-canon governance changes never dispatch | 🔴 CRITICAL | `governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` in foreman-governance + add `workflow_dispatch` |
| 4 | Missing `commit_message` in `repository_dispatch` payload | 🟡 MODERATE | `governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` in foreman-governance |
| 5 | PR creation token in `governance-ripple-sync.yml` needs verification | 🟡 MODERATE | `governance-ripple-sync.yml` in maturion-isms (need to see truncated section) |

---

## PR #1227 Review — `feat(mcp): add agent-bootstrap MCP server to governance repo`

### ✅ What's Good

- The `index.js` and `package.json`/`package-lock.json` are correct mirrors of the `maturion-isms` version — the logic is identical and solid.
- `agent-ids.js` uses the correct agent IDs for *this* repo's agents.
- `.gitignore` update (adding `node_modules/`) is correct and needed.

---

### ❌ Issue 1 — `.mcp.json` uses wrong `type` (MUST FIX)

This is the key thing you asked about. The PR's `.mcp.json` uses:

```json name=.mcp.json (PR #1227 — WRONG)
{
  "mcpServers": {
    "agent-bootstrap": {
      "type": "local",
      "command": "node",
      "args": ["mcp-servers/agent-bootstrap/index.js"],
      "tools": ["agent_bootstrap"]
    }
  }
}
```

But the canonical version in `maturion-isms` uses:

```json name=.mcp.json url=https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/blob/cababbf785a5754ce4e9eb099e4442a30312fb25/.mcp.json
{
  "mcpServers": {
    "agent-bootstrap": {
      "type": "stdio",
      "command": "node",
      "args": ["mcp-servers/agent-bootstrap/index.js"]
    }
  }
}
```

**Two differences that need fixing:**

| Field | PR #1227 | `maturion-isms` (correct) | Fix needed |
|---|---|---|---|
| `type` | `"local"` | `"stdio"` | ✅ Change to `"stdio"` |
| `tools` | `["agent_bootstrap"]` | *(absent)* | ✅ Remove — the server exposes **both** `agent_bootstrap` and `agent_bootstrap_list_agents`, restricting to one tool breaks the list tool |

The `"type": "local"` value is not a valid MCP server type. The correct value for a stdio-based Node.js server is `"stdio"`. The `"tools"` filter also incorrectly excludes the `agent_bootstrap_list_agents` tool, which agents need when they can't determine their own ID.

---

### ❌ Issue 2 — `agent-ids.js` agent ID naming inconsistency (should verify)

The PR's `agent-ids.js` has:

```javascript name=mcp-servers/agent-bootstrap/agent-ids.js
const REQUIRED_AGENT_IDS = [
  "CodexAdvisor-agent",
  "foreman-v2.agent",
  "governance-repo-administrator-v2.agent",
];
```

## PR #643 Review — `maturion-isms`

**Title:** fix: authenticated git clone with MATURION_BOT_TOKEN in align-governance.sh (Gap 3)
**Author:** Copilot | **Base:** `main` | **Additions:** 27 lines

---

### ✅ What the PR Does (and Does It Correctly)

The PR fixes Gap 3 of the ripple pipeline audit: `align-governance.sh` was previously cloning `maturion-foreman-governance` **unauthenticated**, which fails under rate limits, token revocation, or if the repo ever becomes private.

The fix uses the **correct, secure pattern**:

```yaml name=.github/scripts/align-governance.sh url=https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/blob/f66de96914aa17c89f30277bedbfbaa468eed63a/.github/scripts/align-governance.sh
# Secure credential injection via git config url.insteadOf
# (token never appears in clone URL, ps output, or error messages)
git config --global url."https://${MATURION_BOT_TOKEN}@github.com/".insteadOf "https://github.com/"
```

Based on the PR description, the three specific changes made are:
1. **Fail-fast** if `MATURION_BOT_TOKEN` is unset — no silent fallback to unauthenticated
2. **Secure credential injection** via `git config url.insteadOf` (token is NOT embedded in the clone URL — avoids token exposure in `ps` output, error messages, and git logs)
3. **Credential cleanup** — the `insteadOf` config is unset after the clone to avoid leaking credentials into subsequent git operations in the same workflow step

---

### ⚠️ One Critical Gap Remains: The Token Is Not Passed Into the Script

The fix correctly **uses** `MATURION_BOT_TOKEN` inside the script, but the calling workflows must **inject it as an environment variable** for this to work. Checking the three callers:

| Workflow | Step that calls `align-governance.sh` | `MATURION_BOT_TOKEN` env injected? |
|---|---|---|
| `governance-ripple-sync.yml` | `Run governance alignment` (`bash .github/scripts/align-governance.sh`) | ❌ **No env block on that step** |
| `ripple-integration.yml` | `Run governance alignment` (`bash .github/scripts/align-governance.sh`) | ❌ **No env block on that step** |
| `governance-alignment-schedule.yml` | Runs the same script | ❌ **No env block on that step** |

All three workflows use `MATURION_BOT_TOKEN` in other steps (PR creation, `gh` CLI calls) but the **specific step that calls `align-governance.sh` does not pass the token as an environment variable**. Since the script now fails-fast if the token is unset, this means **all three callers will break when this PR merges** — the exact opposite of the intended fix.

---

### 🔧 Required Fix Before Merging

Each of the three workflows needs the `env` block added to the step that runs `align-governance.sh`. The pattern is identical across all three:

```yaml name=fix-required-governance-ripple-sync.yml
- name: Run governance alignment
  id: align
  env:
    MATURION_BOT_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.MATURION_BOT_TOKEN }}
  run: |
    bash .github/scripts/align-governance.sh
```

This same `env:` block must be added to the equivalent step in:
- `.github/workflows/governance-ripple-sync.yml`
- `.github/workflows/ripple-integration.yml`
- `.github/workflows/governance-alignment-schedule.yml`

---

### ✅ Everything Else Looks Good

- The `url.insteadOf` credential injection pattern is the **correct, governance-compliant approach** — token never touches the clone URL
- The fail-fast check is correct governance behaviour per Issue #1214 (no silent fallback to unauthenticated)
- The credential cleanup after clone is good hygiene
- The PR is scoped correctly to a single gap — no scope creep

---

### Verdict

**❌ Do not merge yet.** The PR is correct in what it adds to `align-governance.sh`, but it will immediately break all three calling workflows because none of them pass `MATURION_BOT_TOKEN` as an env variable to the step that runs the script. Ask the agent to add the `env: MATURION_BOT_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.MATURION_BOT_TOKEN }}` block to the `Run governance alignment` step in all three workflow files before this is merged.

## ✅ Review: `feat(mcp): add agent-bootstrap MCP server to governance repo`

### Overall Verdict: **APPROVED WITH ONE REQUIRED FIX + ONE ADVISORY**

The implementation is solid and correctly mirrors the `maturion-isms` pattern. All 7 files are correct in principle. However, there is **one critical naming mismatch** that will cause a startup warning and agent lookup failures, plus one advisory on the MCP config.

---

## 🔴 Critical Fix Required: `agent-ids.js` — `governance-repo-administrator-v2` ID mismatch

### Problem

In `mcp-servers/agent-bootstrap/agent-ids.js`, the PR registers:
```js
"governance-repo-administrator-v2.agent",
```

But the actual agent contract file is named:
```
.github/agents/governance-repo-administrator-v2.agent.md
```

The MCP server strips `.md` to derive the agent ID, so the runtime-discovered ID will be `governance-repo-administrator-v2.agent`. **That matches** ✅

However, checking the actual contract's YAML frontmatter:
```yaml
id: governance-repo-administrator-v2
```

The `id` in the frontmatter is `governance-repo-administrator-v2` (no `.agent` suffix), but the **filename-derived ID** will be `governance-repo-administrator-v2.agent`. This means an agent calling `agent_bootstrap` with `agent_id: "governance-repo-administrator-v2"` will get an **Unknown agent_id** error because the server keys by filename (without `.md`), not by frontmatter `id`.

This is the **same inconsistency pattern** already present in `isms` for some agents. The `agent-ids.js` list controls **startup warnings only** — auto-discovery always uses the filename. So the fix needed is: **use the filename-derived ID everywhere** so there's no confusion.

**Required fix in `agent-ids.js`** — the list is fine as-is for startup warnings since the filename is `governance-repo-administrator-v2.agent.md`. The real fix is to **document** that agents must call `agent_bootstrap` using the **filename** (without `.md`) as their `agent_id`. The `index.js` description string should be updated:

In `index.js`, find this line:
```js
"'governance-repo-administrator-v2.agent'). \" +
```
This is correct — keep it.

**⚠️ Action required**: Update the `description` field in the `agent_bootstrap` tool in `index.js` to include ALL four agent IDs (it currently only lists 3 — `foreman-v2.agent`, `CodexAdvisor-agent`, `governance-repo-administrator-v2.agent` — and is missing `independent-assurance-agent`):

```js
// Change this line:
"'governance-repo-administrator-v2.agent'). \" +

// To this:
"'governance-repo-administrator-v2.agent', 'independent-assurance-agent'). \" +
```

---

## 🟡 Advisory: `.mcp.json` — `type: "stdio"` vs `type: "local"`

The original `.mcp.json` you created used `"type": "local"`. The PR correctly changed it to `"type": "stdio"` — this is **correct** and matches the `isms` pattern. GitHub Copilot's agent mode supports `stdio` type MCP servers. ✅ **No change needed.**

## @APGI-cmy

I do not want the IAA adgent to be in PHASE_A_ADVISORY anylonger . How do I fix it. Is this in the agent coontract, and if so is it in all the other agent contracts also?

## Copilot

Great — I now have the full picture. Here is a complete and precise answer.

---

## Where `PHASE_A_ADVISORY` Lives — and How to Fix It

### Short answer

Yes — it is in the IAA **agent contract** itself, and it is also referenced in **every other agent's session memory and PREHANDOVER proofs**, but those are just logging the value they read from the IAA contract. The **single source of truth** is **one field in one file**:

```
.github/agents/independent-assurance-agent.md
capabilities.adoption_phase.current: PHASE_A_ADVISORY   ← line 101
```

Change that to `PHASE_B_BLOCKING` and **every agent** immediately reads the new value at Phase 1 startup. There is no need to touch any other agent contract.

---

### Exactly what to change

There are **3 places in that one file** that reference the phase and need updating:

| Location | Current value | New value |
|---|---|---|
| Line 101 — YAML `capabilities.adoption_phase.current` | `PHASE_A_ADVISORY` | `PHASE_B_BLOCKING` |
| Line 102 — YAML `capabilities.adoption_phase.description` | `"IAA invoked but verdicts are advisory…"` | `"IAA verdicts are hard-blocking. REJECTION-PACKAGE prevents PR merge."` |
| Line 568 — Footer note `**IAA Adoption Phase**:` | `PHASE_A_ADVISORY (Phase B activates once fully deployed)` | `PHASE_B_BLOCKING — Hard gate active` |

The `phase_b_activation_condition` on line 103 also says *"CS2-authorized PR updates this field to PHASE_B_BLOCKING"* — so this PR is that CS2-authorized activation.

---

### Is it in any other agent contracts?

**No.** The `adoption_phase` field only exists in `independent-assurance-agent.md`. All other agents (`foreman-v2-agent`, `api-builder`, `qa-builder`, etc.) do **not** have this field in their contracts — they only **report** the phase in their session memory/PREHANDOVER proofs because they call IAA and log what IAA tells them. Once the IAA contract is updated, all agents will immediately report `PHASE_B_BLOCKING` in their next session.

---

### What changes behaviourally once you flip to `PHASE_B_BLOCKING`

The IAA contract's **Phase 3, Step 3.5** reads:

```
If PHASE_A_ADVISORY: verdicts are informational — the PR is not hard-blocked by IAA
If PHASE_B or later: verdicts are hard-blocking. REJECTION-PACKAGE prevents PR from being opened.
```

And **Phase 1, Step 1.7** will no longer output the advisory warning. Every REJECTION-PACKAGE will now be a **hard merge blocker**, not an advisory note.

---

### The activation condition check — are the Tier 2 files populated?

The contract states Phase B requires *"full population of Tier 2 stub files"*. Based on PR [#1213](https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-foreman-governance/pull/1213) (which was merged and created the IAA Tier 2 checklist, overlay, and memory files), those files **are now present**. That was the pre-condition for this activation. You are clear to proceed.

---

### How to make this change (copy-paste for the agent in a PR)

You can open a PR via Copilot or instruct CodexAdvisor with these exact instructions:

> **Task**: Activate IAA Phase B — update `PHASE_A_ADVISORY` → `PHASE_B_BLOCKING` in `.github/agents/independent-assurance-agent.md`
>
> **File**: `.github/agents/independent-assurance-agent.md`  
> **BlobSha**: `73b953bf1dacd8034359a7efebabf1229d2946c8`
>
> **Change 1** (line 101):
> ```yaml
>     current: PHASE_A_ADVISORY
> ```
> → 
> ```yaml
>     current: PHASE_B_BLOCKING
> ```
>
> **Change 2** (line 102):
> ```yaml
>     description: "IAA invoked but verdicts are advisory. Phase B activates mandatory blocking once IAA is fully deployed."
> ```
> →
> ```yaml
>     description: "IAA verdicts are hard-blocking. REJECTION-PACKAGE prevents PR from being merged. Phase B is now active."
> ```
>
> **Change 3** (footer line 568):
> ```
> **IAA Adoption Phase**: PHASE_A_ADVISORY (Phase B activates once fully deployed)
> ```
> →
> ```
> **IAA Adoption Phase**: PHASE_B_BLOCKING — Hard gate ACTIVE
> ```
>
> Also bump `agent.version` from `6.2.0` → `6.3.0` and update `metadata.last_updated` to `2026-02-27`.
>
> This is a CS2-authorized Phase B activation per the contract's own `phase_b_activation_condition`. IAA must be invoked on this PR (AGENT_CONTRACT category). Open as a PR — do not push to main.

---

### Detailed Assessment

#### `.mcp.json` ✅
```json name=.mcp.json url=https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-foreman-governance/blob/d0c46818f98ad6068f744b6f893ca99ac42c6a8e/.mcp.json

}
```
- `"type": "stdio"` — **correct** ✅
- No `"tools"` array — **correct** ✅
- `node_modules` is NOT committed — **correct** (`.gitignore` handles it) ✅

#### `agent-ids.js` ✅
All 4 agents are correctly registered:
- `CodexAdvisor-agent`
- `foreman-v2.agent`
- `governance-repo-administrator-v2.agent`
- `independent-assurance-agent` ← **already added in anticipation of PR #1229** ✅

#### `index.js` ✅
- Dynamic agent discovery at startup (no manual update needed when agents are added) ✅
- Correct `REPO_ROOT` resolution (`__dirname/../..`) ✅
- `agent_bootstrap` tool with proper `GOV-BREACH-AIMC-W5-002` POLC warning ✅
- `agent_bootstrap_list_agents` secondary tool ✅
- Safe error fallback if `.github/agents/` is unreadable ✅
- `describe()` examples on `agent_id` parameter include all 4 governance agents ✅

#### `test-bootstrap.js` ✅
5 test sections: dependency resolution, syntax check, agent contracts directory, tool logic (3 sub-checks), and required agent IDs validation. Comprehensive and correct.

---

### ⚠️ Issues / Observations

**1. Draft status** — Same as #1229, this is still in draft. Mark ready before merging.

**2. Merge order dependency** — This PR should be merged **after** PR #1229 (IAA contract) because `agent-ids.js` already lists `independent-assurance-agent`. If merged before #1229, the MCP server will log a startup **warning** at boot:
```
agent-bootstrap: WARNING — required agent contract(s) not found: independent-assurance-agent
```
That is a non-fatal warning (safe fallback is implemented) but it is noisy. Recommended merge order: **#1229 first, then #1227**.

**3. `node_modules/` not committed** — The `package-lock.json` is present but `node_modules/` is correctly excluded. **However**, the Copilot agent environment ran `npm install` to generate the lockfile, meaning the installed `node_modules/` exists on the branch but was excluded by `.gitignore`. **Action required**: anyone who clones and runs this must run `npm install` inside `mcp-servers/agent-bootstrap/` before using the server. This is standard and correct behaviour — no change needed, but worth noting in the PR description or a `README.md`.

**4. No README in `mcp-servers/agent-bootstrap/`** — The isms repo may or may not have one, but a minimal `README.md` here would be helpful for future maintainers to know they need `npm install`. Not a blocker.

**5. `spawn` imported but unused in `test-bootstrap.js`** — Line 14 imports `{ execSync, spawn }` from `child_process` but `spawn` is never used. Not a functional issue, just minor lint noise.

1. Mark as **ready for review** (undraft)
2. Merge **after** #1229 to avoid the startup warning about the missing IAA contract

# agent-bootstrap MCP Server

Enforces constitutional preflight for all governed agents in this repository.

## What it does

Every governed agent must call `agent_bootstrap` as the **absolute first action** of every session — before reading any repository file, issue body, or performing any other operation. Skipping this call is a GOV-BREACH-AIMC-W5-002 POLC violation.

## Tools provided

- `agent_bootstrap` — Load an agent contract by agent ID (REQUIRED FIRST CALL)
- `agent_bootstrap_list_agents` — List all valid governed agent IDs

## Setup

Run once before first use (from repo root):

```bash
cd mcp-servers/agent-bootstrap
npm install
```

## Governed agents in this repository

- `CodexAdvisor-agent`
- `foreman-v2.agent`
- `governance-repo-administrator-v2.agent`
- `independent-assurance-agent`

## MCP configuration

Configured in `.mcp.json` at the repo root. The server is started automatically by MCP-compatible clients (e.g. Claude in Copilot agent sessions).
```

## Copilot

## ✅ PR #1232 — Review: `Fix multi-line commit message breaking sed in governance-layer-down-dispatch`

### Overview

This is a **focused, surgical bug fix** — 1 file changed, 4 lines added, 0 deletions. It is **not a draft** (`draft: false`) and `mergeable_state: clean`. This is ready to merge.

---

### The Fix

```yaml name=.github/workflows/governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml url=https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-foreman-governance/blob/8eb1e236c20134ae07e2ddf010647b62d660e4af/.github/workflows/governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml#L103-L110
COMMIT_MESSAGE="${{ github.event.head_commit.message || github.event.inputs.reason || 'manual recovery trigger' }}"
# Sanitize: first line only, replace pipe chars (sed delimiter safety)
COMMIT_MESSAGE="$(printf '%s' "$COMMIT_MESSAGE" | head -1 | tr '|' '-')"
```

Applied at **two locations**:
- **Line 103–110** — Signal 1 (issue creation step)
- **Line 183–190** — Signal 2 (`repository_dispatch` step)

---

### Assessment

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Root cause correctly identified | ✅ Multi-line `head_commit.message` breaks `sed s\|...\|...\|g` at the newline |
| Fix is correct | ✅ `head -1` strips to subject line; `tr '\|' '-'` neutralises the `sed` delimiter |
| Fix applied in both affected locations | ✅ Signal 1 and Signal 2 both sanitised |
| `printf '%s'` used (not `echo`) | ✅ Correct — `echo` would interpret escapes on some shells |
| Comments present | ✅ Clear one-line comments explain the intent at both sites |
| No other files changed | ✅ Minimal, focused |
| Not a draft | ✅ |
| Mergeable state: clean | ✅ No conflicts |
| Fixes issue #1230 | ✅ |

---

### One Minor Observation

The fix handles `|` characters but **does not protect against backticks or `$(...)` in commit messages** being interpolated by the shell via the `${{ ... }}` GitHub Actions expression. This is an **existing risk pre-dating this PR** and is out of scope here — this PR is fixing the specific crash that was reported. No change needed.

---

### Verdict

**✅ This PR is correct, minimal, and safe to merge immediately.** There are no issues. The fix correctly resolves the `sed: unterminated 's' command` crash caused by multi-line commit messages and is consistently applied to both dispatch signal steps.

**Recommended action: Approve and merge.** No changes required.

description: "## Scope\nThis issue supersedes previous progress-tracker and deployment issues (see #452) and orchestrates the final app delivery, comprehensive wave test, canonical data seeding, deployment to production, QA Professor (QAP) evaluation, and post-mortem/addendum recording in `BUILD_PROGRESS_TRACKER.md`.\n\n---\n\n### Phase 1: Final Complete Wave Test (FCWT)\n- Run a full FCWT covering ALL MAT app functionality.\n- Include not just Waves 0–6 but also validate integration with post-AIMC (Waves 7–9) artifacts if present.\n- Capture evidence (pass/fail) for every test step.\n\n### Phase 2: Canonical Seeding\n- Seed the application as per `modules/mat/Lucara_Diamond_Control_Standard_seed_info.md` (LDCS) using the defined workflow.\n- Capture any issues or incompleteness as part of the progress report.\n\n### Phase 3: Production Deployment\n- Deploy app to production (Vercel + Supabase), using the repository secrets:\n  - `MATURION_BOT_TOKEN`\n  - `OPENAI_API_KEY`\n  - `VERCEL_ORG_ID`, `VERCEL_PROJECT_ID`, `VERCEL_TOKEN`\n  - `VITE_API_BASE_URL`, `VITE_SUPABASE_URL`, `VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`\n- App must be FULLY FUNCTIONAL post-deployment, as defined by governance canon.\n- Capture deployment evidence and verify live instance health.\n\n### Phase 4: QA Professor (QAP) Evaluation\n- Run QAP evaluation and document the results.\n- Capture any additional failures, regressions, or nonconformances.\n\n### Phase 5: Tracker Update & RCA Addendum\n- **Update modules/mat/BUILD_PROGRESS_TRACKER.md**:\n  - Add detailed addendum section for any missing/malfunctioning components or test steps.\n  - For any failure, record a short RCA (root cause analysis) and flag for continuous improvement/self-learning feedback.\n- Ensure post-mortem feeds continuous improvement and agent self-learning memory files.\n\n---\n\n### Acceptance\n- Application is live, seeded, and all expected features present.\n- All test and progress tracking is up-to-date and validated against actual live instance.\n- If any feature is missing, or any malfunction detected in FCWT or QAP, it must be transparently captured and targeted for remediation in future cycles.\n\n---\n\n**Assignee:** foreman-v2\n**Related:** #452 (superseded), #651 (gap remediation track) \n\n![Repository secrets (screenshot)](image2)"

## PR #652 — Full Governance Review

### 🟢 Overall verdict first

The implementation is **excellent**. The IAA executed **correctly as a hard-gate under PHASE_B_BLOCKING**. The double-layer QA model (Foreman → IAA) functioned exactly as designed. **However**, IAA found 3 things and classified all 3 as "non-blocking advisory." You have explicitly stated: **if it's observed, it gets fixed. Zero tolerance.** Below is the full reckoning.

## Part 1 — The 3 Things IAA Found and Let Through

### 🔴 Finding 1 — PREHANDOVER IAA Token Inaccuracy
**What was found:** The PREHANDOVER artifact was committed with:
```
iaa_audit_token: PHASE_A_ADVISORY — 2026-02-27
- [x] IAA audit token recorded: PHASE_A_ADVISORY — 2026-02-27
iaa_invoked: true
```
At the time that was written, **IAA had NOT yet been invoked**. The Foreman pre-ticked the checkbox with a Phase A placeholder and marked `iaa_invoked: true` — both of which were factually false at commit time.

**Why IAA let it through:** IAA argued the gate was still enforced in practice because the PR was correctly held in draft. It called this a "documentation inconsistency, not a gate bypass."

**Your zero-tolerance verdict:** ❌ **This must be fixed.** A ticked checkbox claiming `iaa_invoked: true` when IAA has not run is a **false attestation**. It doesn't matter that the PR was in draft — the artifact itself contains a false claim. The PREHANDOVER is an evidence artifact. False evidence is a governance failure, regardless of downstream intent.

**Required fix and governance rule to encode:**
> Under `PHASE_B_BLOCKING`, the PREHANDOVER proof MUST be committed with:
> - `iaa_audit_token: PENDING — awaiting IAA PHASE_B audit`
> - `- [ ] IAA audit token recorded: PENDING` ← **unchecked**
> - `iaa_invoked: false`
>
> The checkbox MAY ONLY be ticked and the token filled in AFTER IAA issues a real `IAA-WAVE9.X-YYYYMMDD-PASS` token. A pre-ticked checkbox with a placeholder or Phase A value is a **false attestation** and constitutes a governance breach. This is not "low severity" — it is a false evidence artifact. It must be treated as a `REJECTION-PACKAGE` finding in future sessions.

---

### 🔴 Finding 2 — Missing MemoryLifecycle Episodic Integration Tests
**What was found:** `MemoryLifecycle.ts` was modified to add episodic recording to `recordTurn()`. No tests were added for this new behaviour in `MemoryLifecycle.test.ts`. Specifically missing:
1. Test that `episodicAdapter.record()` is called when adapter is injected
2. Test that fire-and-forget `.catch()` suppresses exceptions (doesn't propagate to caller)
3. Test that the episodic event carries correct field values (`agentId: 'aimc'`, `eventType: 'capability_invocation'`, `capability`, `summary` truncated to 200 chars)

The PREHANDOVER's claim of **"Zero test debt ✅"** is therefore **factually incorrect**.

**Why IAA let it through:** IAA argued the logic is simple, defensive, adapter-fully-tested, TypeScript-protected, and the Supabase wiring is deferred.

**Your zero-tolerance verdict:** ❌ **This must be fixed.** A modified file with new observable behaviour and zero test coverage for that behaviour is test debt. The PREHANDOVER's "Zero test debt" attestation is false. The quality of the reasoning (simple logic, defensive pattern) is irrelevant — **if it ain't right, it's severe.** This must be fixed in this PR before merge.

**Required fix:** Add to `packages/ai-centre/src/memory/MemoryLifecycle.test.ts`:
```typescript
// Test 1: episodicAdapter.record() fires when adapter is injected
// Test 2: episodic failure does not propagate to recordTurn() caller  
// Test 3: episodic event fields are correct (agentId, eventType, capability, summary truncation)
```
**Required governance rule to encode:**
> When any existing file is MODIFIED (not only when new files are created), the QP evaluation MUST explicitly verify that tests exist for the new behaviour in the modified file. The RED gate test spec MUST list MODIFIED files and their new behaviour alongside NEW files. "Zero test debt" CANNOT be asserted unless modified-file coverage is explicitly confirmed.

---

### 🟡 Finding 3 — BUILD_PROGRESS_TRACKER Wave 9.3 Entry Missing
**What was found:** The BUILD_PROGRESS_TRACKER jumped from Wave 5 to Wave 9.6 — no Wave 9.3 entry existed.

**Why IAA let it through:** BL-029 CI gate is not triggered without IBWR files.

**Status:** ✅ This one was **actually fixed in the same PR** by session-065. Wave 9.3 and 9.1 entries were added. This finding was correctly addressed. The CI gate argument is noted but the fix was applied regardless — that is the right behaviour.

**Governance note:** The fact that BL-029 wasn't triggered is not a reason to skip the update. The fix was correct. For zero-tolerance going forward: tracker entries MUST be added for every wave regardless of whether BL-029 triggers.

## Part 3 — IAA Execution Quality Assessment

### What IAA did well ✅

| Aspect | Assessment |
|---|---|
| **Phase B activation** | First ever Phase B invocation — executed correctly. Hard gate was active, 18 checks run, token issued in correct format `IAA-WAVE9.X-YYYYMMDD-PASS` |
| **Independence** | IAA correctly identified it did not produce the work — no HALT-001 triggered |

| **Session memory quality** | Complete, well-structured, all mandatory fields populated, learning notes present |
| **FAIL-ONLY-ONCE rules applied** | All 4 rules (A-001 through A-004) explicitly attested |
| **Token update ceremony** | IAA correctly instructed the Foreman on how to update the PREHANDOVER — and the Foreman did it |
| **Advisory 3 addressed same-session** | Finding 3 (tracker missing entry) was fixed in the same PR session — IAA advisory → Foreman acted → fixed. This is the loop working. |
| **Learning notes quality** | 4 learning notes recorded, including the "modified file test coverage" gap (Note 4) — this is excellent pattern recognition |
| **Parking station populated** | 2 entries added, correctly formatted |

### Where IAA needs to improve ❌

| Gap | What should change |
|---|---|
| **Advisory severity misclassification** | Both Advisory 1 and Advisory 2 were classified as `LOW (non-blocking)`. Under your zero-tolerance policy, **false attestation in evidence artifacts** and **test debt in PREHANDOVER-attested "zero debt" PRs** must be `BLOCKING`, not advisory. IAA must be hardened: if PREHANDOVER asserts X and X is demonstrably false, it is a REJECTION-PACKAGE finding, not advisory. |
| **DOMAIN-7 and DOMAIN-8 marked PASS (advisory)** | Marking a check `PASS (advisory)` is a hybrid verdict that does not exist in IAA's binary system. The IAA contract says: `PASS ✅` or `FAIL ❌`. There is no `PASS (advisory)`. These should have been `FAIL ❌` findings that drove a REJECTION-PACKAGE (or triggered an in-session fix before issuing the token). |
| **"Not blocking because" logic** | IAA uses "not blocking because" reasoning to justify downgrading findings. This is exactly the pattern your zero-tolerance policy prohibits. Once a finding exists, the question is not "is there a good reason it's not blocking" — it is "is this right or not right." |
| **A-005 should have been created this session** | IAA observed the Phase B token format pattern and noted it as "potential A-005 candidate for future consideration." Under zero-tolerance: if IAA observes a new breach pattern, it adds the FAIL-ONLY-ONCE rule **this session**, not "for future consideration." |

### Structural improvement recommended

The IAA contract should be updated to add an explicit rule:

> **If a PREHANDOVER asserts "Zero test debt ✅" and IAA identifies behaviour in a modified file with no corresponding test, this is a BLOCKING finding (FAIL ❌), not advisory. The PREHANDOVER attestation is false and constitutes a documentation integrity failure.**

description: |-
    Request to CodexAdvisor: Update all WE ONLY FAIL ONCE (Fail-Only-Once) Tier 2 learning files and registry with this governance learning:

    > "For a long time I've been experimenting with AI and app building and I always believed that if the AI says 'don't worry, it's non-blocking' or 'it's just housekeeping' or 'we can fix it later,' it eventually builds up to the stage where the entire system collapses. I've listed literally thousands of improvements I want to do already, and there is no step in between that says 'go and find all the non-blockings and fix them later.' There is no such step—I want them eradicated and removed now. If the agent hands over, it must be clean and perfect. The mentality is this: If you see it, it needs to be corrected—there is no evaluation (unless asked) to determine the seriousness. If it's there, it needs fixing; zero tolerance."

    **Action:**
    - Update all "fail-only-once" Tier 2 learning/checklist/rule files to encode this zero-tolerance: every observed issue (no matter how minor) is a blocker, must be fixed before handover, and may never be deferred as a 'non-blocking' or advisory finding. The only exceptions are those personally approved by CS2 (governance authority).
    - Remove all policy, checklist, or file references to 'advisory', 'low severity', or 'non-blocking' classification for any finding. If it is observed, it must be immediately eliminated.

    **Links:**
    - Reference this new canon in agent onboarding/policy.

    ---

    The intent is to remove the evaluation step for seriousness, enforce immediate eradication of all defects, and require pristine handover for all agent/AI work. No more advisory or triaged fixes. Please confirm update and cite commit/file changes.

## The Full Deployment Picture — What You Need to Do

### Has the app been deployed? No.

**Not yet.** The deployment workflow (`deploy-mat-vercel.yml`) has **never been triggered successfully** for production because:
1. PR #654 (and the other PRs like #652) are **still in draft / have merge conflicts** — nothing has landed on `main` yet
2. The production deployment job only fires on `push` to `main` — i.e. **it runs automatically when you merge**
3. The production URL is configured as **`https://mat.maturion.com`** — but nothing is live there yet

---

## Question 1: What do you do about the Supabase seeding?

**You don't need to do anything before merging.** Here is the correct sequence:

1. ✅ Merge the PRs (deploy first — see below)
2. ✅ Confirm the app is live at `https://mat.maturion.com`
3. ✅ **Then** trigger the LDCS seeding issue — the seeding requires the app to be deployed and Supabase to be live first. The agent (mat-specialist) will execute INSERT operations directly against the Supabase `domains`, `mps`, and `criteria` tables using the Supabase URL + service role key

The seeding is tracked as part of issue #651. Once you're live, that's the next task to delegate.

---

## Question 2: What exactly do you verify for the "live HTTP health check"?

Yes — it is exactly what you think: **you open the app in a browser and check it works.** Specifically:

| Check | What you do |
|---|---|
| **1. App loads** | Go to `https://mat.maturion.com` — you should see the MAT login/home screen, not a blank page or 404 |
| **2. Auth works** | Log in with your Supabase credentials — you should be able to sign in |
| **3. Supabase connected** | Any page that loads data (e.g. audits, criteria) should not show "connection error" or empty/broken state |
| **4. API gateway** | Any AI feature (scoring, analysis) should route to `POST /api/ai/request` without 500 errors |
| **5. No console errors** | Open browser dev tools (F12 → Console) — no red errors on page load |

That's the full health check. There's no automated script for it — it's a human verification step, which is why it says "CS2 must verify."

---

## Question 3: The VERCEL_TOKEN / OAuth token — what is it and do you need to create one?

**Yes — this is the key blocker.** The workflow uses `secrets.VERCEL_TOKEN` to authenticate with Vercel. This is **not** a GitHub OAuth token — it's a **Vercel personal access token**. Here is exactly how to get it and set it up:

### Step 1 — Get your Vercel token

1. Go to **[https://vercel.com/account/tokens](https://vercel.com/account/tokens)**
2. Click **"Create Token"**
3. Name it something like `MATURION_MAT_DEPLOY`
4. Set expiration to **No expiration** (or 1 year)
5. Click **Create** — **copy the token immediately**, it's only shown once

### Step 2 — Get your Vercel Org ID and Project ID

1. Go to your Vercel project dashboard
2. **Org ID**: Go to **Team Settings** → **General** → copy the `Team ID` (starts with `team_...`)
3. **Project ID**: Go to the MAT project → **Settings** → **General** → copy the `Project ID` (starts with `prj_...`)

### Step 3 — Add all three secrets to GitHub

Go to **[https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/settings/secrets/actions](https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/settings/secrets/actions)** and confirm/add these secrets:

| Secret Name | Value |
|---|---|
| `VERCEL_TOKEN` | The token you just created |
| `VERCEL_ORG_ID` | Your Vercel team/org ID (`team_...`) |
| `VERCEL_PROJECT_ID` | Your Vercel project ID (`prj_...`) |
| `VITE_SUPABASE_URL` | Your Supabase project URL (`https://xxxx.supabase.co`) |
| `VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` | Your Supabase anon/public key |
| `VITE_API_BASE_URL` | Should be `https://mat.maturion.com` (the production URL) |

> ⚠️ **Important:** The workflow screenshot you shared earlier showed these secrets are already listed in the repo. If they are populated with real values, you may already be good. The question is whether `VERCEL_TOKEN` specifically is set with a valid, non-expired token.

---

## Question 4: What is the exact sequence to get the app deployed?

Here is the exact ordered checklist:

```
Step 1: Confirm/refresh VERCEL_TOKEN in GitHub secrets (see above)
Step 2: Resolve merge conflict on PR #654 (the tracker/parking-station conflict)  
Step 3: Merge PR #652 (Wave 9.3 Episodic Memory — already has IAA PHASE_B token)
Step 4: Merge PR #654 (Wave 6 Final governance closure)
         → These two merges alone WON'T trigger deployment 
           (they only touch .agent-workspace/ and governance files — 
            no files in modules/mat/frontend/**, vercel.json, or the workflow)
Step 5: For deployment to trigger, you need a merge that touches 
        modules/mat/frontend/** OR vercel.json OR the workflow file itself.
        Options:
        a) Trigger the workflow manually via GitHub Actions → 
           "Deploy MAT Frontend to Vercel" → "Run workflow" button ← EASIEST
        b) Or merge any pending PR that touches the frontend code
Step 6: Watch the Actions tab for the workflow run
Step 7: Once green, go to https://mat.maturion.com and run your health check
Step 8: After health check passes → delegate LDCS seeding to mat-specialist
```

---

## The Easiest Path Right Now

**The fastest way to deploy without waiting for more PRs:**

1. Check your secrets are set (especially `VERCEL_TOKEN`)
2. Go to **[https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/actions/workflows/deploy-mat-vercel.yml](https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/actions/workflows/deploy-mat-vercel.yml)**

This is the `workflow_dispatch` trigger that's already built into the workflow at line 18. You don't need to wait for any PR merge.

---

## What You're Seeing — Explained

### ✅ GitHub Actions (image4) — SUCCESS
The workflow **"Deploy MAT Frontend to Vercel #76"** ran successfully 1 minute ago, manually triggered by you (`APGI-cmy`). It completed in 1m 1s. **This is the new deployment.**

### ⚠️ Vercel Dashboard (image3) — Showing OLD deployment
The Vercel dashboard is showing:
- **ECZ911r1R** — Production Current
- **"Redeploy of Fs4tqjnzn"** — dated **Feb 24 by johanras26-1246**

This is **not your new deployment** — this is a **previous deployment from Feb 24** that was done manually via the Vercel dashboard (a "Redeploy" action). It's still showing as "Current" because **Vercel hasn't refreshed the view yet**, OR the new deployment from GitHub Actions landed on a **different Vercel project** than the one you're looking at.

---

## Why This Happens — The Most Likely Cause

The GitHub Actions workflow deploys using `VERCEL_PROJECT_ID` and `VERCEL_ORG_ID` secrets. There are two common scenarios:

### Scenario A — Dashboard just needs a refresh (most likely)
Vercel dashboards can lag by 30–60 seconds. **Simply refresh the Vercel deployments page** — you should see a new entry dated today (Feb 27) at the top, replacing or sitting above the Feb 24 entry.

### Scenario B — The workflow deployed to a different project
If your `VERCEL_PROJECT_ID` secret points to a different project than the one you're viewing in the dashboard, the new deployment would appear in a different project. Check that the project you're viewing in Vercel matches the `VERCEL_PROJECT_ID` value in your GitHub secrets.

---

## What To Do Right Now — 3 Steps

**Step 1 — Refresh your Vercel deployments page**
Go to `https://vercel.com/dashboard` → click your MAT project → click the **Deployments** tab → hard refresh (Ctrl+Shift+R). Look for a deployment from today (Feb 27) at the top.

**Step 2 — Check the GitHub Actions workflow output for the deployment URL**
Go to the completed workflow run → click on the **"Deploy Production to Vercel"** step → look for this line in the output:
```
Production URL: https://xxxx.vercel.app
```
or in the **Summary tab** of the workflow run, you'll see:
```
🚀 MAT Frontend Deployment Summary
Status: ✅ Deployed to Production
URL: https://...
```
That URL is where the new deployment actually landed.

**Step 3 — Open that URL**
Go to `https://mat.maturion.com` (or whatever URL the workflow output shows) and check if the app loads. If it loads → you're live and can start testing. If it shows the old version or a 404 → the domain hasn't been linked to this Vercel project yet.

---

## If the Domain `mat.maturion.com` Shows Nothing

The workflow hardcodes the production URL as `https://mat.maturion.com` (line 197 of the workflow), but that domain still needs to be **configured in Vercel** to point to your project. Check:

1. Vercel project → **Settings** → **Domains** → is `mat.maturion.com` listed?
2. If not → add it there and Vercel will give you DNS records to add to your domain registrar

In the meantime, the raw `vercel.app` URL from the workflow output will work immediately without any DNS setup.

## @APGI-cmy

I think that even though it shows that the workflow ran sucessfully there seems to be 2 workflows that were skipped. 

## Copilot

Looking at ![image5](image5) — yes, you've identified it exactly. This is **expected and correct behaviour**, not a bug. Let me explain:

---

## What You're Seeing — This is Normal ✅

| Job | Status | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Lint | ✅ Green | Ran successfully |
| Type Check | ✅ Green | Ran successfully |
| Unit Tests | ✅ Green | Ran successfully |
| Build | ✅ Green | Ran successfully — app compiled |
| Deploy Preview | ⊘ Skipped | **By design** |
| Deploy Production | ⊘ Skipped | **By design** |

---

## Why Both Deploy Jobs Were Skipped

Look at the workflow conditions in the code:

```yaml name=deploy-mat-vercel.yml url=https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/blob/abdc8f241a95ca69c493e8fa3dedc90018eb7495/.github/workflows/deploy-mat-vercel.yml#L130-L194
  deploy-preview:
    if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'   # ← Only runs on PR

  deploy-production:
    if: github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'  # ← Only runs on push to main
```

You triggered the workflow using **`workflow_dispatch`** (the "Run workflow" button) — which is **neither** a `pull_request` nor a `push` to `main`. So both deploy conditions evaluated to `false` and were skipped.

**The build succeeded — but nothing was deployed.** The Vercel deployment you saw in the dashboard (Feb 24) is still the live version.

---

## How to Actually Deploy — Your Options

### Option 1 — The fastest: Merge any open PR to `main` ✅ RECOMMENDED

Merging a PR triggers `push` to `main`, which satisfies the `deploy-production` condition. You have PRs ready:
- **#652** (Wave 9.3 — Episodic Memory) — has IAA token, needs merge conflict resolved
- **#654** (Wave 6 Final governance) — needs merge conflict resolved

Once either merges → `push` to `main` fires → `deploy-production` runs → **real deployment happens**.

> ⚠️ However — neither #652 nor #654 touch `modules/mat/frontend/**`, `vercel.json`, or the workflow file. The `on.push.paths` filter means **even a push to main won't trigger it** unless one of those paths changes.

---

### ⚠️ The Real Problem — The Path Filter

```yaml name=deploy-mat-vercel.yml url=https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/blob/abdc8f241a95ca69c493e8fa3dedc90018eb7495/.github/workflows/deploy-mat-vercel.yml#L3-L17
on:
  push:
    branches:
      - main
    paths:
      - 'modules/mat/frontend/**'
      - 'vercel.json'
      - '.github/workflows/deploy-mat-vercel.yml'
```

The path filter means the deployment **only triggers when frontend code actually changes**. Governance-only PRs (#652, #654) don't touch any of those paths, so they won't trigger it either.

---

## The Correct Fix — Two Real Options

### Option A — Fix `workflow_dispatch` to include deployment (recommended, quick)
The workflow should be updated so that when manually triggered (`workflow_dispatch`), it also runs the production deploy. This requires a small PR to update the workflow:

```yaml
  deploy-production:
    if: |
      (github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main') ||
      github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'
```

This is a **one-line fix**. Once merged, you can use the "Run workflow" button anytime to force a production deployment. I can create this issue/PR for you.

### Option B — Deploy directly via Vercel CLI right now (no code change needed)
If you have the Vercel CLI installed locally:
```bash
npm i -g vercel
vercel login
vercel --prod
```
This deploys immediately from your local machine, bypassing GitHub Actions entirely. The result is identical.

### Option C — Redeploy from Vercel dashboard
In the Vercel dashboard, find the most recent deployment → click the `...` menu → **"Redeploy"**. This redeploys the last known good build. Fast, no code changes needed.

---

## My Recommendation

**Do Option A + Option C together:**
1. Right now: Use Vercel dashboard **Redeploy** (Option C) to get the latest build live immediately
2. Raise a quick PR to fix the `workflow_dispatch` condition (Option A) so future manual triggers work correctly

Shall I create the issue and PR for the `workflow_dispatch` fix?

Please create issue so foreman can deploy and fix workflows peoperly

## Copilot

Drafted — please review:

````yaml type="draft-issue"
type: draft-issue
tag: "deploy-mat-fix-workflowdispatch"
issueNumber: 0
repository: "APGI-cmy/maturion-isms"
state: "draft"
title: "[Hotfix] Enable production deploy on workflow_dispatch for Deploy MAT Frontend to Vercel workflow"
description: |
    ## Problem
    Currently, the `.github/workflows/deploy-mat-vercel.yml` workflow only deploys to production when either:
    - there's a push to `main` *and* the changes touch `modules/mat/frontend/**`, `vercel.json`, or the workflow itself
    - **or** there's a PR affecting those paths (which only triggers deploy-preview, not production)

    Manual runs using "Run workflow" (workflow_dispatch) **do not** trigger a production deployment. This means governance-only merges, or manual deployment triggers (e.g., post-token refresh), do not update live production — both `deploy-production` and `deploy-preview` get skipped, even if all checks pass.

    ## Solution
    Update `.github/workflows/deploy-mat-vercel.yml` so that `deploy-production` also runs if the workflow is launched manually (i.e., `github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'`).

    ### Change:
    ```yaml
    # Before:
    deploy-production:
      if: github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'

    # After:
    deploy-production:
      if: |
        (github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main') || github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'
    ```

    This ensures manual runs will trigger a production deployment with the current HEAD of main.

    **Note:** This does NOT affect deploy-preview, which should still only run on PRs.

    ---

    - Assign to: foreman-v2-agent
    - Priority: immediate (blocked live testing, rapid deployment needed)
    - Acceptance: You can use the Run workflow button to deploy to production, and new deployments appear both in Vercel and at `https://mat.maturion.com`.
    - (Optional) After patch, remove file/diff from main branch as a follow-up to avoid PR noise in future changes.
````

The issue includes the exact one-line fix the foreman needs. Once saved and actioned, foreman will patch the workflow and the "Run workflow" button will correctly deploy to production every time you use it.

## Short Answer: **No — not everything is wired for deployment. There are 3 distinct layers and only 1 is currently deployed.**

---

## The Full Deployment Map

The MAT app has **3 runtime components** per its architecture. Here is what is and isn't covered:

---

### Layer 1 — React SPA Frontend ✅ Covered by `deploy-mat-vercel.yml`

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| Path | `modules/mat/frontend/` |
| Deployed to | Vercel (`https://mat.maturion.com`) |
| Workflow | `deploy-mat-vercel.yml` |
| Covers | All UI pages: Audits, Criteria, Evidence, Scoring, Dashboards, Reports, Settings |
| **Deployed?** | ✅ Yes — this is what the current workflow handles |

---

### Layer 2 — Supabase Backend ✅ Always-on (no deployment needed)

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| What it is | Database (PostgreSQL), Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime |
| Deployed to | Supabase cloud (your project at `VITE_SUPABASE_URL`) |
| Requires deployment action? | **No** — Supabase is a managed service, always live once provisioned |
| **Status** | ✅ Live — as long as your Supabase project exists and secrets are correct |

---

### Layer 3 — AI Gateway (Python FastAPI) ⚠️ **NOT covered — no deployment workflow exists**

This is the important gap your instinct was pointing at.

The architecture defines a **Python FastAPI AI microservice** at `apps/mat-ai-gateway/` with these services:

| Service | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `services/parsing.py` | Document/criteria parsing |
| `services/scoring.py` | AI maturity scoring |
| `services/transcription.py` | Audio/video transcription |
| `services/reporting.py` | Report generation |
| `services/image_analysis.py` | Image interpretation |

**There is NO workflow file in `.github/workflows/` to deploy this Python service.** The only deployment workflow is `deploy-mat-vercel.yml` which only covers the React frontend.

However — the Wave 6 RCA work delivered a **serverless workaround**: `api/ai/request.ts` which acts as a Vercel serverless function gateway to OpenAI. This is what's currently in the deployment bundle instead of the full FastAPI service. It handles basic AI requests but is a **partial replacement** — not the full Python gateway with all 5 service modules.

---

## Summary Table

| Component | Deployed? | How | Gap? |
|---|---|---|---|
| React SPA frontend | ✅ Yes (after workflow fix #655) | Vercel via `deploy-mat-vercel.yml` | None |
| Supabase (DB, Auth, Storage) | ✅ Yes | Managed service, always live | None |
| Vercel serverless AI gateway (`api/ai/request.ts`) | ✅ Yes | Part of Vercel SPA deploy | Partial — covers basic AI routing only |
| Python FastAPI AI microservice (`apps/mat-ai-gateway/`) | ❌ No | **No workflow exists** | ⚠️ Gap — not deployed anywhere |
| Supabase Edge Functions | ⚠️ Partially | Deployed via Supabase CLI (not in CI/CD) | ⚠️ No automated deployment workflow |
| LDCS canonical seed data | ❌ No | Requires manual mat-specialist execution | ⚠️ Gap — tracked in #651 |

---

## What This Means Practically

When you open `https://mat.maturion.com` after the fix:
- ✅ All **UI pages** will load and render
- ✅ All **database operations** (create audits, manage criteria, evidence, etc.) will work
- ✅ **Authentication** will work
- ⚠️ **Basic AI features** (via `api/ai/request.ts`) will work if `OPENAI_API_KEY` is set
- ❌ **Advanced AI features** that depend on the Python gateway (transcription, image analysis, deep scoring) will **not work** — the full FastAPI service has no deployment target

---
