## Code Review — File by File

### `apps/mat-ai-gateway/main.py` ✅

Well structured. Startup validation fires at import time — correct. `RuntimeError` with the variable name in the message — satisfies `test_error_message_names_the_missing_variable`. CORS via `CORS_ORIGINS.split(",")` — correct. Router import after app creation with `# noqa: E402` — acceptable pattern given the env-var guard must precede all imports.

**One observation (non-blocking):** `allow_credentials=True` combined with `allow_origins=["*"]` is technically invalid per the CORS spec — browsers will reject credentialed cross-origin requests when `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. This works fine for server-to-server calls, but if the frontend ever sends `credentials: 'include'`, it will silently fail. For pilot use this is fine; flag for Wave 11 hardening when `CORS_ORIGINS` is tightened to specific origins.

---

### `apps/mat-ai-gateway/routers/ai_routes.py` ✅

Service singletons at module level — correct (code review fix pass 3 applied). All 5 routes present as POST. Pydantic request models are well-typed. `ReportRequest.format` field — this shadows Python's built-in `format`, which was the original code review finding. The fix renamed the **parameter** passed to `_generator.generate()` as `report_format` while leaving the Pydantic field as `format` (which is fine — `format` as a Pydantic field name is valid). ✅

**One observation:** `ScoreRequest.evidence` accepts `list[Union[str, dict]] | None` but the route handler does:
```python
evidence = request.evidence if isinstance(request.evidence, list) else [request.evidence]
```
If `request.evidence` is `None`, this becomes `[None]` — a list containing `None` — which gets passed to `_scorer.score()`. The stub doesn't care, but the real implementation will. Flag for Wave 11 wiring when real OpenAI calls are made.

---

### `apps/mat-ai-gateway/services/*.py` ✅

All 5 service stubs are structurally correct: importable, one public class each, correct method signatures matching router calls, appropriate docstrings with architecture `§` references. All return consistent `{"status": "queued", "task_id": "stub", ...}` shapes. Interface contracts are well-defined for future Wave 11 wiring.

---

### `apps/mat-ai-gateway/Dockerfile` ✅

Multi-stage build is correct. `python:3.11-slim` — appropriate for production. `HEALTHCHECK` uses `python -c "import urllib.request; urllib.request.urlopen('http://localhost:8000/health')"` — avoids curl dependency on slim image. ✅

**One observation:** The `HEALTHCHECK` does not set `--retries` (defaults to 3). For a FastAPI app that can take 2–3 seconds to start under load, `--start-period` is missing. Consider adding `--start-period=10s` in Wave 11 to prevent false unhealthy flags during container startup. Non-blocking.

---

### `apps/mat-ai-gateway/requirements.txt` ✅

```
fastapi>=0.115.0
uvicorn[standard]>=0.32.0
httpx>=0.28.0
pydantic>=2.9.0
pydantic-settings>=2.6.0
python-multipart>=0.0.12
```

`pydantic-settings` is included but not actually used in `main.py` (env-var validation is via `os.environ.get` loop, not `BaseSettings`). Non-blocking — `pydantic-settings` is a lightweight transitive dep and will be needed when the real settings model is implemented. `python-multipart` is correctly included for FastAPI form/file upload support.

---

### `.github/workflows/deploy-mat-ai-gateway.yml` ✅

**All 5 jobs have explicit `permissions` blocks** — ✅ (this was the code review fix in commit `7f92cfb`). `deploy-preview` and `deploy-production` both use `permissions: {}` (empty = no permissions) — correct for placeholder deploy steps that only `echo`.

**Observations:**

1. **`flake8` excludes only `__pycache__`** — the `tests/` directory is not excluded. Flake8 will lint test files too. This is not wrong, but `test_environment.py` uses a bare `except Exception` which flake8 may flag as `E722` (bare except) or `BLE001` (blind exception). The `# noqa: BLE001` comments are present in the test file — but `flake8` without the `flake8-bugbear` plugin won't know about `BLE001`. With standard flake8, these are fine. ✅

2. **No `pip cache`** — the lint and test jobs install dependencies fresh every run. For a pilot this is fine, but consider adding `actions/cache` for `pip` when CI runtime becomes important. Non-blocking.

3. **Test step does not set env vars** — the `Run tests` step does not set `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `SUPABASE_URL`, or `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`. The conftest **monkeypatches these in session scope**, so tests pass without real values. This is correct by design — tests are isolated from real credentials. ✅

4. **`build` job does not push to a registry** — just builds locally. This means the built image is lost when the runner exits and cannot be used by `deploy-production`. This is acceptable for the current placeholder state, but when real deployment is wired, `build` must push to ECR/GCR/GHCR and `deploy-*` must pull from there. Flag this explicitly in the runbook (it is somewhat implied by AG.6 but not stated).

---

### `DEPLOYMENT_RUNBOOK_MAT.md` (AG.1–AG.9) ✅

Comprehensive and well-referenced. AG.4 references `cp .env.example .env` — but there is **no `.env.example` file** in the PR. This will confuse developers following the runbook. Minor gap — should be added in Wave 11, or the runbook instruction should say "create manually" (which it already does in the alternative `or create manually with required vars`). ✅ acceptable as-is.

### Tests — All 6 files ✅

**`test_environment.py`** — 12 tests. Great question. Let me look at your current ripple/layer-up automation setup to give you an accurate answer.Good — I now have the full picture. Let me give you a clear, honest answer.

---

## Your Question: Should This Be Automated?

**Short answer: YES, it is supposed to be automated — and the plumbing largely exists. But there is a critical gap specifically for the *layer-up* direction (isms → governance), which is why you're being asked to do it manually.**

Here is exactly what is wired and what is not:

---

## The Full Ripple Architecture

```
maturion-foreman-governance  ←──────── SOURCE OF TRUTH (canon)
         │
         │  On merge to main
         ▼
governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml   ← EXISTS ✅
         │
         │  Sends repository_dispatch: governance_ripple
         ▼
maturion-isms (consumer)
         │
         ├── governance-ripple-sync.yml   ← EXISTS ✅ (receives layer-DOWN)
         └── governance-alignment-schedule.yml ← EXISTS ✅ (hourly fallback)
```

**Layer-DOWN (governance → isms) is automated.** When something merges in `maturion-foreman-governance`, it dispatches a ripple and `maturion-isms` has a listener to receive it. That is why you see those 3 auto-generated `[Ripple]` PRs (#671, #672, #673) — the downward automation is working.

---

## The Gap: Layer-UP Direction

```
maturion-isms (consumer)
         │
         │  PR #691 merges with new OVL-CI-004 + §Known Failure Modes
         ▼
layer-up-dispatch.yml   ← EXISTS ✅ in maturion-isms
         │
         │  Triggers ONLY when an issue is labeled
         │  with BOTH: [layer-up] + [governance-improvement]
         ▼
Creates issue in maturion-foreman-governance
         │
         ▼
governance-layer-up-intake.yml   ← EXISTS ✅ in maturion-foreman-governance
         │
         ▼
governance-layer-up-close-loop.yml  ← EXISTS ✅ in maturion-foreman-governance
```

**The layer-up pipeline is also automated — but it is triggered by an issue label, NOT by a PR merge.** This is by design, because layer-ups require a deliberate human decision: *"This learning is good enough to promote to canon."* The system does not auto-promote every PR because not everything in `maturion-isms` belongs in the canonical governance repo.

---

## Why You Were Asked to Create It Manually

My suggestion in the PR review was imprecise — I apologise for the confusion. The correct way to trigger the automated pipeline is:

1. **Create an issue in `maturion-isms`** describing what from PR #691 should be promoted (e.g., `OVL-CI-004` + `§Known Failure Modes`)
2. **Apply both labels**: `layer-up` + `governance-improvement`
3. **`layer-up-dispatch.yml` fires automatically** and creates the intake issue in `maturion-foreman-governance`
4. **`governance-layer-up-intake.yml`** processes it and the governance-repo-administrator agent reviews
5. **After CS2 approves and merges** in the governance repo, **`governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` fires** and ripples it back down to all consumer repos automatically

**You do not need to manually create a PR in `maturion-foreman-governance`.** You just need to create and label the issue in `maturion-isms` and the rest is automated.

---

## Summary Table

| Direction | Workflow | Trigger | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Layer-DOWN: governance → isms | `governance-layer-down-dispatch.yml` → `governance-ripple-sync.yml` | PR merged in governance repo | ✅ Fully automated |
| Layer-UP: isms → governance | `layer-up-dispatch.yml` → `governance-layer-up-intake.yml` | Issue labeled `layer-up` + `governance-improvement` in isms | ✅ Automated — **but you must create and label the issue to start it** |
| Layer-UP close loop | `governance-layer-up-close-loop.yml` | Issue closed in governance repo | ✅ Automated |

**The human step is intentional** — it is the deliberate promotion decision. Once you create and label the issue, the rest is hands-off.

## Issue 1 — Consumer Side (`maturion-isms`)

````yaml type="draft-issue"
type: draft-issue
tag: "auto-layer-up-autoloop-infra"
issueNumber: 0
repository: "APGI-cmy/maturion-isms"
state: "draft"
title: "[Governance] Automate Layer-Up Trigger: PR Merge/Comment → Layer-Up Issue Created"
description: |
    ## Problem
    Currently, layer-up (promotion from consumer repo to canonical governance) is **semi-automated**: after a PR merges in maturion-isms, a human must create a new issue and apply `layer-up` and `governance-improvement` labels to trigger cross-repo escalation. This slows down self-improving automation and adds manual burden.

    ## Proposal
    Implement infra so that **closing a PR with a special comment (e.g. `Auto layer up approved` or `Layer up approved`), or simply merging a PR, will automatically open the needed `layer-up` issue** (containing a cross-link to the PR and a summary of what should be canonized). The issue should be labeled automatically (`layer-up`, `governance-improvement`) to trigger the existing `layer-up-dispatch.yml` escalation. If automation fails, retain human fallback: manual issue creation is always allowed.

    ## Implementation Requirements
    - [ ] Add workflow to listen for `pull_request.closed` or new comment event with flag text ("Auto layer up approved", etc)
    - [ ] On PR merge or flagged comment, auto-create issue summarizing the canonizable governance improvement, with correct labels and PR cross-link
    - [ ] Auto-label issue `layer-up` & `governance-improvement`
    - [ ] If the PR/issue cross-link or content matches an existing governance canon, escalate for reconciliation review (by governance-repo-admin agent or CS2)
    - [ ] If it is additive and not in conflict, proceed with escalation to canonical
    - [ ] Update issue template/instructions for reviewers: If you see the human-in-the-loop approval phrase, proceed
    - [ ] Existing manual labeling remains as fallback

## Issue 2 — Governance Side (`maturion-foreman-governance`)

````yaml type="draft-issue"
type: draft-issue
tag: "auto-layer-up-intake-infra-gov"
issueNumber: 0
repository: "APGI-cmy/maturion-foreman-governance"
state: "draft"
title: "[Governance Infra] Accept & Triage Auto-Layer-Up Escalation Issues — Automate Canonical Close-Loop"
description: |
    ## Problem
    Governance improvements layered-up from consumer repos (`maturion-isms`, etc) should be able to flow fully automatically with a human-in-the-loop (HITL) approval comment at the PR merge step (e.g. `Auto layer up approved`). The canonical repo should receive auto-generated layer-up issues, containing metadata and traceability (cross-link to PR, cross-link to isms issue), with content ready for triage. Currently, human must create/label the intake issue.

    ## Proposal
    - Listen for layer-up issues created from consumer (isms) repo automation
    - Ingest issue, check for explicit hitl comment (e.g. "Layer up approved") from repo maintainer or CS2 auth
    - If comment/approval present and content is not in conflict, proceed with canonization (create PR, route to CS2 if agent contract or constitutional change)
    - If in conflict with canon, escalate for reconciliation/CS2 review
    - Log/track loop closure for full automation paths
    - Document workflow & governance logic in strategy docs

    ## Implementation Requirements
    - [ ] Add auto-intake workflow to listen for new issues labeled `layer-up` + `governance-improvement` created via API or GitHub Actions
    - [ ] Parse key metadata: originating PR, repo, explicit approval phrase, governance delta
    - [ ] If explicit approval, evaluate governance improvement for conflict/additivity
       - [ ] If additive, proceed; if conflicting, escalate for reconciliation
    - [ ] If HITL approval absent or ambiguous, assign to governance-repo-admin for manual triage
    - [ ] Log all automation ops; gap detection for loop closure (future: alert if loop not closed)
    - [ ] Update documentation (`GOVERNANCE_RIPPLE_SYSTEM.md`, `LAYER_UP_PROTOCOL.md`) and strategy diagrams

    ## Acceptance Criteria
    - [ ] Layer-up issues landed from consumer repo are audited for HITL approval & value-add
    - [ ] Escalation and close-loop logging in place
    - [ ] Automation is visible and documented
    - [ ] Manual triage/fallback always available

    ---
    **Authority**: Governance-Repo-Administrator, CS2 (for constitutional/agent file changes)
````

---

## How This Closes the Loop

Once these are implemented, the full automated cycle becomes:

```
PR in maturion-isms
    │
    │  You comment: "Auto layer up approved"
    ▼
[NEW] pr-layer-up-trigger.yml fires on comment/merge
    │  Auto-creates issue with labels layer-up + governance-improvement
    ▼
layer-up-dispatch.yml fires (already exists ✅)
    │  Creates intake issue in maturion-foreman-governance
    ▼
[UPGRADED] governance-layer-up-intake.yml
    │  Reads HITL approval phrase from issue body
    │  If additive → proceeds to canonize
    │  If conflict → escalates to CS2
    ▼
governance-layer-up-close-loop.yml fires on close (already exists ✅)
    │  Dispatches layer-down back to maturion-isms
    ▼
governance-ripple-sync.yml fires (already exists ✅)
    └  Loop closed ✅
```

**Your human approval is the PR comment** — one phrase, and the rest is fully hands-off. Would you like me to include the relevant existing workflow files as context for the implementing agent when these are submitted?

## What the Fix Actually Is

Currently line 107 of the template reads:

```markdown name=prehandover-template.md url=https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/blob/d209cc3ae6e3dfde9f4ca244536582b9ef46e5cc/.agent-workspace/foreman-v2/knowledge/prehandover-template.md#L107-L117

<!-- Paste the COMPLETE raw output from task(agent_type: "independent-assurance-agent") here -->
<!-- A PREHANDOVER proof with a blank or placeholder IAA response section is a HANDOVER BLOCKER -->
<!-- IAA bare PHASE_A_ADVISORY without this section = INC-IAA-SKIP-001 breach -->

[IAA agent output pasted verbatim here — the ASSURANCE-TOKEN or REJECTION-PACKAGE block]
```

The session-020 suggestion is to **add an explicit post-ASSURANCE-TOKEN ceremony step** explaining:
1. Set `iaa_audit_token: PENDING` before invoking IAA (not pre-filled `-PASS`)
2. After IAA issues ASSURANCE-TOKEN: update token to `IAA-NNN-YYYYMMDD-PASS`
3. Append the verbatim ASSURANCE-TOKEN block
4. Commit — then open the PR

## Copilot

## PR #698 Review — `feat: automate layer-up trigger — approval phrase on PR creates layer-up issue automatically`

**Overall verdict: ✅ APPROVE — clean, well-scoped CI automation PR with correct security posture. No conflicts with existing governance.**

---

### Summary

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| **PR** | [#698](https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/pull/698) |
| **Fixes** | Issue [#697](https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/issues/697) |
| **Type** | CI/CD workflow (new) + documentation updates |
| **Files changed** | 3 |
| **Additions / Deletions** | +388 / -6 |
| **State** | Draft (mergeable, no conflicts) |
| **Author** | Copilot (foreman-v2-agent) |

---

### File-by-File Assessment

#### 1. `.github/workflows/layer-up-trigger.yml` ✅ NEW — 320 lines

This is the core deliverable. Assessment:

**Trigger logic:**
- `issue_comment.created` + `pull_request.closed` (merged-only) — correct, minimal event scope
- Approval phrase regex: `(auto\s+layer[\s-]up\s+approved|layer[\s-]up\s+approved)` — case-insensitive, handles hyphenated and spaced variants — **well done**
- Guards correctly against: non-PR issue comments (`ISSUE_IS_PR != true`), closed-but-not-merged PRs — both exit cleanly with `should_trigger=false`

**Security posture:**
- ✅ All user-supplied values (`PR_BODY`, `COMMENT_BODY`, `PR_TITLE`) are passed via `env:` blocks, never inline-interpolated in `run:` scripts — correct injection prevention per Maturion CI security policy
- ⚠️ **One remaining inline expression** — in the `issue_comment` path, the URL is constructed as:
  ```yaml name=layer-up-trigger.yml url=https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/blob/cb3c060e5d217be5f4bb50932061c783782e3775/.github/workflows/layer-up-trigger.yml#L84
  OUT_PR_URL="https://github.com/${{ github.repository }}/pull/${ISSUE_NUMBER}"
  ```
  `${{ github.repository }}` is a GitHub-controlled value (not user-supplied), so this is **not a security risk** — it is safe. However, for full stylistic consistency with the security comment at the top of the file, it could optionally be moved to an `env:` variable. **Non-blocking.**

**Idempotency:**
- ✅ `dup_check` step queries for open layer-up issues matching `"PR #${PR_NUMBER}"` in title — prevents duplicates correctly
- The title format of the auto-created issue is `[Layer-Up] Auto-triggered: ${PR_TITLE} (PR #${PR_NUMBER})` — consistent with the search pattern ✅

**Issue body construction:**
- Uses `printf` with all values from `env:` — safe
- PR body is truncated to 3000 chars via `head -c 3000` — prevents oversized issue bodies ✅
- `EOF_PR_BODY` heredoc pattern for multi-line `GITHUB_OUTPUT` — correct ✅

**Label handling:**
- Uses `gh label create ... 2>/dev/null || echo` — gracefully handles pre-existing labels ✅
- Labels `layer-up` and `governance-improvement` hardcoded with consistent colours

**Feedback loop:**
- `Comment on originating PR` step runs `if: always()` — fires even on failure, giving the human a fallback notice ✅
- Three distinct comment states: created, duplicate, failed — all handled ✅

**Permissions:**
```yaml name=layer-up-trigger.yml url=https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/blob/cb3c060e5d217be5f4bb50932061c783782e3775/.github/workflows/layer-up-trigger.yml#L30-L33
permissions:
  contents: read
  issues: write
  pull-requests: read
```
Minimal required permissions — **correct**. No `write-all` or over-scoped tokens.

**Token fallback:**
- `secrets.MATURION_BOT_TOKEN || github.token` — consistent with existing workflow patterns ✅

---

#### 2. `docs/GOVERNANCE_RIPPLE_SYSTEM.md` ✅ MODIFIED — v1.0.0 → v1.1.0

- Component count 7→8 ✅
- New Component 7 "Layer-Up Trigger Automation" correctly placed before the old "Documentation" component (now Component 8) ✅
- Section 3 renamed from "Manual Trigger (Testing)" to "Automated Layer-Up Trigger (Upward Direction)" and old section 3 renumbered to Section 4 — **correct**, the manual trigger section remains untouched in substance
- Version history entry `v1.1.0 (2026-03-01)` added ✅
- The component description accurately reflects what the workflow does — no exaggerations or gaps

---

#### 3. `governance/canon/LAYER_UP_PROTOCOL.md` ✅ MODIFIED — v1.0.0 → v1.1.0

- Phase 1 now explicitly documents both **Automated Path** and **Manual Path** — the manual path is preserved intact ✅
- The automated path steps (1–4) correctly describe the trigger → detect → create → dispatch chain ✅
- Version bumped to `1.1.0`, `Updated` date added — correct ✅
- **No existing protocol content was removed or altered** — purely additive ✅

---

### Governance Alignment Check

| Criterion | Status |
|---|---|
| Does NOT oppose existing governance | ✅ — purely additive; manual path preserved |
| `layer-up-dispatch.yml` unchanged — no new dependency | ✅ — issue labelling is the interface |
| `GOVERNANCE_RIPPLE_SYSTEM.md` and `LAYER_UP_PROTOCOL.md` both updated | ✅ |
| Conflict-detection instructions present in auto-created issues | ✅ — CS2 escalation path documented |
| Human fallback preserved | ✅ — explicitly documented in both docs |
| Idempotent (no duplicate issues) | ✅ |
| Minimal permissions scope | ✅ |
| Injection-safe | ✅ (with one minor stylistic note, non-blocking) |
| Issue [#697](https://github.com/APGI-cmy/maturion-isms/issues/697) acceptance criteria met | ✅ All 5 criteria addressed |

---

### Minor Observations (Non-blocking)

1. **`pr_author` is `"unknown"` for `issue_comment` trigger** — this is documented in a code comment and is a known limitation of the `issue_comment` event (GitHub doesn't expose the PR author there). Acceptable. Could be enriched in future by fetching PR details via `gh pr view`, but that would add a step to the hot path — not worth it now.

2. **`ISSUE_NUM` extraction relies on `grep -oE '[0-9]+$'`** on the URL returned by `gh issue create` — this is a common and reliable pattern on GitHub URLs, but slightly fragile if the URL format changes. Low risk. A future improvement could use `gh issue create --json number`.

3. **No test/validation workflow for `layer-up-trigger.yml` itself** — this is expected for a governance automation workflow (not a code workflow). Manual verification on first use is the appropriate path here.

---

### Verdict

**✅ APPROVE and merge.** This PR correctly implements exactly what issue #697 requested, adds to rather than opposes existing governance, maintains security best practices, and documents the change in both relevant governance documents. The workflow fits perfectly into the existing `layer-up-dispatch.yml` pipeline without modification to that workflow.

This is a net positive for the self-improving governance vision — hands-off layer-up escalation from PR comment is now operational.
